where
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2}).
Then, by equation (
9.64
),

\begin{array}[]{cllll}T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x)=P_{Y_{2}}\left(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{2})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x\right).\end{array}

By Proposition
9.15
, every
Q_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
which minimises
(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q))
satisfies the following equation (see equation (
9.29
)),

(G-Q_{2})V_{0}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
x\end{pmatrix}=W_{0}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
F_{2}x\end{pmatrix},

(9.67)

for some
F_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})+C(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{T}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
. This implies that

(G-Q_{2})\bar{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x,

(9.68)

for
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2}).
Hence

T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x)=P_{Y_{2}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x).

(9.69)

Furthermore,

\begin{array}[]{clll}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\Gamma_{2}(\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x)&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}[(G-Q_{2})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x].\end{array}

Hence, by equation (
9.68
),

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\Gamma_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x\right)=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x).

(9.70)

To show commutativity of the right hand square in the diagram (
9.66
), we need to prove that, for every
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})
,

T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x)=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\Gamma_{2}(\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}x).

(9.71)

By equations (
9.69
) and (
9.70
), it is equivalent to
show that

P_{Y_{2}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x).

(9.72)

Therefore, we need to show that

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)\in Y_{2}

and that

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x-\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)

is orthogonal to
Y_{2}.

By Proposition
9.24
,
\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)
is indeed an element of
Y_{2}.

Furthermore,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x-\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}[\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x-P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)]\\
&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\perp}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)\end{array}.

Let us show that
\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\perp}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)
is orthogonal to
Y_{2}
.

It is so if and only if

\left\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\perp}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x),\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}g\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0\quad\text{for every}\;g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.

(9.73)

Let
\Phi=P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\perp}}(\beta_{0}\beta_{1}F_{2}x)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
By Proposition
9.25
,

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2}).

(9.74)

Then, by Proposition
3.12
, assertion (
9.73
) is equivalent to the following assertion

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}\;d\theta=0

for every
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},
which in turn, by Proposition
5.1
, is equivalent to the assertion

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
0&1&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}\;d\theta=0

for every
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
The latter statement is equivalent to the assertion

\begin{array}[]{clll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&-\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&-\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),g(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\;d\theta=0\end{array}

for every
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},
which in turn is equivalent to the statement that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}g^{*}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta})&-g^{*}(e^{i\theta})\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta})(e^{i\theta})\\
&-g^{*}(e^{i\theta})\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{1}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta})\;d\theta=0\end{array}

for every
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
Equivalently

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}g^{*}(e^{i\theta})\bigg(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{0}^{T}(e^{i\theta})-\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{1}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\bigg)\Phi(e^{i\theta})\;d\theta=0

for every
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
if and only if

\bigg(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{0}^{T}(e^{i\theta})-\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{1}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\bigg)\Phi(e^{i\theta})

is orthogonal to

H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},
which occurs if and only if

\left(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}\right)\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m}).

By Lemma
9.22
,

\left(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}\right)\Phi=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Phi.

Recall that, by assertions (
9.74
),
\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2}),
and so

\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Thus the right hand square in the diagram (
9.66
) commutes, and so the diagram (
9.66
) commutes.

(iv) By Proposition
9.15
,

F_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)}).

Thus, by Hartman’s Theorem, the Hankel operator
H_{F_{2}}
is compact. By (iii),

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\circ(M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}H_{F_{2}}M_{\alpha_{0}^{T}\alpha_{1}^{T}})\circ(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}=T_{2}.

By (i) and (ii), the operators
M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}},\;M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries. Hence
T_{2}
is a compact operator.

(v) Since diagram (
9.66
) commutes and
the operators
M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}},\;M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries,

\|T_{2}\|=\|\Gamma_{2}\|=\|H_{F_{2}}\|=t_{2}.

∎

Lemma 9.27
.

In the notation of Theorem
9.26
, let
v_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
and
w_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
be such that
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for the operator
T_{2}
corresponding to
\|T_{2}\|.
Then

(i) there exist
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
such that
(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for the operator
\Gamma_{2}
;

(ii) for any
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
such that

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x_{2}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\quad\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}y_{2}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2},

the pair
(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{2}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{2}\|.

Proof.

(i) By Theorem
9.26
, the diagram (
9.66
) commutes,
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
is unitary from
\mathcal{K}_{2}
to
X_{2},
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
is unitary from
\mathcal{L}_{2}
to
Y_{2}
and
\|\Gamma_{2}\|=\|T_{2}\|=t_{2}.
Moreover, by the commutativity of diagram (
9.66
), the operator
\Gamma_{2}\colon\mathcal{K}_{2}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}
is compact, hence there exist
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2},

y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
such that
(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{2}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{2}\|=t_{2}.

(ii) Suppose that
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2},y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
satisfy

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x_{2}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}\quad\text{and}

(9.75)

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}y_{2}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2}.

(9.76)

Let us show that
(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{2},
that is,

\Gamma_{2}x_{2}=t_{2}y_{2},\quad\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}x_{2}.

Since diagram (
9.66
) commutes,

T_{2}\circ(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\circ\Gamma_{2},\quad(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}\circ T_{2}^{*}=\Gamma_{2}^{*}\circ(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}.

(9.77)

By hypothesis,

T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2})=t_{2}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2}),\quad T_{2}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})=t_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}).

(9.78)

Thus, by equations (
9.75
), (
9.76
) and (
9.78
),

\begin{array}[]{clllll}\Gamma_{2}x_{2}&=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2})\\
&=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}t_{2}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})\\
&=t_{2}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}y_{2}).\end{array}

Hence

\Gamma_{2}x_{2}=t_{2}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)y_{2}=t_{2}y_{2}.

By equation (
9.75
),

x_{2}=(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}),

and, by equation (
9.76
),

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})=y_{2}.

Thus

\begin{array}[]{clll}\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2}&=\Gamma_{2}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})\\
&=(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}T_{2}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2}),\end{array}

the last equality following by the second equation of (
9.77
). By equations (
9.75
) and (
9.78
), we have

T_{2}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})=t_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2})=t_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x_{2}),

and so,

\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}x_{2}.

Therefore
(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}
. ∎

Lemma 9.28
.

Suppose
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
T_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}.
Let

x_{2}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2},\quad y_{2}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2},

and let

\hat{x}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2},\quad\hat{y}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}.

Then the pair
(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{2}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{2}}\|=t_{2}.

Proof.

Let us first show that
\hat{x}_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}.
Recall that
V_{0}=\big(\begin{matrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{matrix}\big)
and
\tilde{V}_{1}=\big(\begin{matrix}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{matrix}\big)
are unitary-valued, that is,
\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{0}=0,

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}=0,

I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T},

(9.79)

and

I_{n-1}-\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}\bar{\alpha}_{0}=\bar{\alpha}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{T}.

(9.80)

Then

\displaystyle\hat{x}_{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2}-\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}v_{2}-\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}v_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2},

(9.81)

which, by Lemmas
9.4
and
9.18
, implies that
\hat{x}_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2}).
Moreover, by Lemma
9.20
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2}&=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2}\\
&=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2}=x_{2}.\end{array}

Hence

x_{2}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2},

(9.82)

and thus
x_{2}\in\mathcal{K}_{2}.

Next, we shall show that
\hat{y}_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})^{\perp}
and
y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}.
Notice that since
\tilde{W}_{1}^{T}=\big(\begin{matrix}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{1}&\bar{\beta}_{1}\end{matrix}\big)
and
W_{0}^{T}=\big(\begin{matrix}\eta_{0}&\bar{\beta}_{0}\end{matrix}\big)
are unitary-valued,

\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{0}=0,

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}=0,

\big(I_{m-1}-\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\big)=\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}

(9.83)

and

\big(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}\big)=\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}.

(9.84)

We have

\displaystyle\hat{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}w_{2}-\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}w_{2}-\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}w_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}w_{2},

(9.85)

which, by Propositions
9.9
and
9.17
, implies that
\hat{y}_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2})^{\perp}.

By Lemma
9.22
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lllllll}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\hat{y}_{2}&=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}w_{2}\\
&=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2}\vskip 8.61108pt=y_{2}.\end{array}

Hence

y_{2}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}w_{2}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\hat{y}_{2},

(9.86)

and therefore
y_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}.

By Theorem
9.26
, the maps

M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})\to\mathcal{K}_{2},\quad M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2})^{\perp}\to\mathcal{L}_{2},

are unitaries and

H_{F_{2}}=M^{*}_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}\Gamma_{2}M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}.

(9.87)

We need to show that

H_{F_{2}}\hat{x}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{y}_{2},\quad H_{F_{2}}^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.

By equations (
9.81
) and (
9.82
),

x_{2}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2}.

(9.88)

Hence equation (
9.87
) yields

\displaystyle H_{F_{2}}\hat{x}_{2}

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Gamma_{2}\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Gamma_{2}x_{2}.

(9.89)

By Proposition
5.1
(ii),

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x_{2}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\quad\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}y_{2}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2}.

Thus, by Lemma
9.27
,

(x_{2},y_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for the operator
\Gamma_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}=\|\Gamma_{2}\|
, that is,

\Gamma_{2}x_{2}=t_{2}y_{2},\quad\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}x_{2}.

(9.90)

Thus equation (
9.89
) yields

H_{F_{2}}\hat{x}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\Gamma_{2}x_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}t_{2}y_{2}=t_{2}\hat{y}_{2}

as required.
Let us show that
H_{F_{2}}^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.
By equations (
9.85
) and (
9.86
),

y_{2}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}.

(9.91)

By equation (
9.87
),

H_{F_{2}}^{*}=M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}^{*}\circ\Gamma_{2}^{*}\circ M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}.

Hence

\displaystyle H_{F_{2}}^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\Gamma_{2}^{*}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\hat{y}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2},

(9.92)

and, by equations (
9.90
) and (
9.92
),

H_{F_{2}}^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\Gamma_{2}^{*}y_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}t_{2}x_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.

Therefore
(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{2}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{2}}\|=t_{2}.

∎

Proposition 9.29
.

Let
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})
be a Schmidt pair for
T_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}
for some
v_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}),w_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},
let
h_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
be the scalar outer factor of
\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}
, let

x_{2}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2},\quad y_{2}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2},

and let

\hat{x}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{y}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}.

(9.93)

Then

\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|,

\|x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|y_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{2}(z)|

and

\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\xi_{1}(z)\wedge v_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}(z)\wedge w_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{2}(z)|

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.28
,
(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{2}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{2}}\|=t_{2}
(see Theorem
9.26
(v)). Hence

H_{F_{2}}\hat{x}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{y}_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{F_{2}}^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.

Then, by Theorem
4.11
,

\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
.
Notice that, by equations (
9.93
),

x_{2}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2},

and since
\bar{\alpha}_{0}(z),\bar{\alpha}_{1}(z)
are isometric for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T},
we obtain

\|x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}.

Furthermore, by equations (
9.93
),

y_{2}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\hat{y}_{2},

and since
\beta_{0}(z),\beta_{1}(z)
are isometries almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
we have

\|y_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
By equations (
9.25
), we deduce

\|x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=\|y_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}

(9.94)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
By Proposition
5.1
,

\xi_{0}\wedge\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}=\xi_{0}\wedge\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x_{2},\quad\bar{\eta}_{0}\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}\wedge w_{2}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}\wedge y_{2}.

Hence, by Proposition
3.14
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\xi_{1}(z)\dot{\wedge}v_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\xi_{1}(z)\wedge x_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
&=\|x_{2}(z)-\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{1}\langle x_{2}(z),\xi_{i}(z)\rangle\xi_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\end{array}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
. Furthermore

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}(z)\wedge w_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}(z)\wedge y_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&=\|y_{2}(z)-\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{1}\langle y_{2}(z),\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\rangle\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|y_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{array}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
. Thus, by equation (
9.94
),

\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\xi_{1}(z)\wedge v_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}(z)\wedge w_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Recall that
h_{2}
is the scalar outer factor of
\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}
. Hence

\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|,

\|x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|y_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{2}(z)|

and

\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\xi_{1}(z)\wedge v_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\bar{\eta}_{1}(z)\wedge w_{2}(z)\|_{\wedge^{3}\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{2}(z)|

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
∎

Proposition 9.30
.

Let
m,n
be positive integers such that
\min(m,n)\geq 2.
Let
G\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n}).
In line with the algorithm from Subsection
4.2
,
let
Q_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
satisfy

\begin{array}[]{llll}(G-Q_{2})x_{0}&=t_{0}y_{0},\quad(G-Q_{2})^{*}y_{0}&=t_{0}x_{0},\\
(G-Q_{2})x_{1}&=t_{1}y_{1},\quad(G-Q_{2})^{*}y_{1}&=t_{1}x_{1}.\end{array}

(9.95)

Let the spaces
X_{2},Y_{2}
be given by

X_{2}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}),\quad Y_{2}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},

and consider the compact operator
T_{2}\colon X_{2}\to Y_{2}
given by

T_{2}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}x)=P_{Y_{2}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{2})x)

for all
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).

Let
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}w_{2})
be a Schmidt pair for the operator
T_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}=\|T_{2}\|,
let
h_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
be the scalar outer factor of
\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}v_{2}
, let

x_{2}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2},\quad y_{2}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2}

and let

\xi_{2}=\frac{{x}_{2}}{h_{2}},\quad\eta_{2}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{y}_{2}}{h_{2}}.

Then there exist unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{2}
of types
(n-2)\times(n-2)
and
(m-2)\times(m-2)
respectively of the form

\tilde{V}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2}&\bar{\alpha}_{2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{2}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}{\beta}_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}&\bar{\beta}_{2}\par\end{pmatrix},

where
\alpha_{2},\beta_{2}
are inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous and all minors on the first columns of
\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{2}^{T}
are in
H^{\infty}.
Furthermore, the set
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of all level
2
superoptimal error functions for
G
satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{2}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}I_{2}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{2}^{*}\par\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0&0\\
0&0&t_{2}u_{2}&0\\
0&0&0&(F_{3}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{2})\par\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I_{2}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{2}^{*}\end{pmatrix}V_{1}^{*}V_{0}^{*},

for some
F_{3}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)}),
where
u_{3}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{3}}{h_{3}}
is a quasi-continuous unimodular function and
B(t_{2})
is the closed ball of radius
t_{2}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)}).

Proof.

By Theorem
9.26
, the following diagram commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}}&\mathcal{K}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{2}\\
\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{2}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{2}$\hss}&&\hskip 12.91663pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{2}$\hss}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}}&\mathcal{L}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{2}.\end{array}

(9.96)

Recall that the operators
M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}},\;M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries.

By Proposition
8.1
,
T_{2}
is well defined and is independent of the choice of
Q_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
satisfying conditions (
9.95
). Hence we may choose
Q_{2}
to minimise

(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q))
, and then, by Proposition
9.16
, the conditions (
9.95
) hold.

By Lemma
9.27
,
(x_{2},y_{2})
defined above is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{2}
corresponding to
t_{2}
. By Lemma
9.28
,
(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{2}}
corresponding to
t_{2},
where

\hat{x}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2},\quad\hat{y}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}.

We intend to apply Lemma
4.12
to
H_{F_{2}}
and the Schmidt pair

(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
to find unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{2}
such that, for every
\tilde{Q}_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
which is at minimal distance from
F_{2},
a factorisation of the form

F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2}=\tilde{W}_{2}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}u_{2}&0\\
0&F_{3}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{2}^{*}

is obtained, for some
F_{3}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)}).
For this purpose we find the inner-outer factorisations of
\hat{x}_{2}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{2}.

By Lemma
9.29

\|\hat{x}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|\;\text{and}\;\|\hat{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|

(9.97)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Equations (
9.97
) imply that
h_{2}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
is the scalar outer factor of both
\hat{x}_{2}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{2}.

By Lemma
4.12
,

\hat{x}_{2},\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{2}
admit the inner outer factorisations

\hat{x}_{2}=\hat{\xi}_{2}h_{2},\quad\bar{z}\bar{y}_{2}=\hat{\eta}_{2}h_{2},

for some inner
\hat{\xi}_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2}),\hat{\eta}_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2}).
Then

\hat{x}_{2}=\hat{\xi}_{2}h_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2},\quad\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{2}=\hat{\eta}_{2}h_{2}=\bar{z}\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{y}_{2},

from which we obtain

\hat{\xi}_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2},\quad\hat{\eta}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}.

We show that
\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2},\;\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}
are inner in order to apply Lemma
4.12
and obtain
\tilde{V}_{2}
and
\tilde{W}_{2}.
Recall that, by Lemma
9.28
,

x_{2}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*})v_{2}=\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2},\quad y_{2}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T})w_{2}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}w_{2}.

Thus

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2}=\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2},\quad\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{y}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{w}_{2},

and since

\xi_{2}=\frac{x_{2}}{h_{2}},\quad\eta_{2}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{y}_{2}}{h_{2}},

we deduce that the functions

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2}=\frac{\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{2}}{h_{2}},\quad\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}=\frac{\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{z}\bar{w}_{2}}{h_{2}}

are analytic. Furthermore,
\|\xi_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=1
and
\|\eta_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=1
almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and, by equations (
9.97
),

\|\alpha_{1}^{T}(z)\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)x_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=\|\alpha_{1}^{T}(z)\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)v_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|

and

\|\beta_{1}^{T}(z)\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\bar{y}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=\|\beta_{1}^{T}(z)\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\bar{w}_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=|h_{2}(z)|

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Hence

\|\alpha_{1}^{T}(z)\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)\xi_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-2}}=1,\quad\|\beta_{1}^{T}(z)\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\eta_{2}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}=1

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Thus
\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2},\;\beta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}
are inner functions.

By Lemma
4.12
, there exist inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous functions
\alpha_{2},\beta_{2}
of types
(n-2)\times(n-3),(m-2)\times(m-3)
respectively such that the functions

\tilde{V}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{2}&\bar{\alpha}_{2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{2}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}{\beta}_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{2}&\bar{\beta}_{2}\par\end{pmatrix}

are unitary-valued with all minors on the first columns in
H^{\infty}.

Furthermore, by Lemma
4.12
, every
\hat{Q}_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
which is at minimal distance from
F_{2}
satisfies

F_{2}-\hat{Q}_{2}=\tilde{W}_{2}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}u_{2}&0\\
0&F_{3}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{2}^{*},

for some
F_{3}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)})
and for the quasi-continuous unimodular function given by
u_{2}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{2}}{h_{2}}.

By Lemma
4.15
, the set

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}=\{F_{2}-\hat{Q}:\hat{Q}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)}),\|F_{2}-\hat{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}=t_{2}\}

satisfies

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}=\tilde{W}_{2}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}u_{2}&0\\
0&(F_{3}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{2})\end{pmatrix}V_{2}^{*},

where
B(t_{2})
is the closed ball of radius
t_{2}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-3)\times(n-3)}).
Thus, by Proposition
9.15
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}
admits the factorisation claimed. ∎

Proposition 9.31
.

Every
Q_{3}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
which minimises

(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{2}^{\infty}(G-Q))

satisfies

(G-Q_{3})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},\quad(G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1,2.

Proof.

By Proposition
9.16
, every
Q_{3}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
that minimises

(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q))

satisfies

(G-Q_{3})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},\quad(G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1.

Hence it suffices to show that
Q_{3}
satisfies

(G-Q_{3})x_{2}=t_{2}y_{2},\quad(G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}x_{2}.

By Theorem
9.26
, the following diagram commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-2})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}}&\mathcal{K}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{2}\\
\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{2}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{2}$\hss}&&\hskip 12.91663pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{2}$\hss}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-2})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}}&\mathcal{L}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{2},\end{array}

where the operator
\Gamma_{2}\colon\mathcal{K}_{2}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}
is given by
\Gamma_{2}=P_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}M_{G-Q_{2}}|_{\mathcal{K}_{2}}
and
F_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
is constructed as follows.

By Lemma
4.12
and Proposition
9.15
, there exist unitary-valued functions

\tilde{V}_{1}=\big(\begin{matrix}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{matrix}\big),\quad\tilde{W}_{1}^{T}=\big(\begin{matrix}\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{1}&\bar{\beta}_{1}\end{matrix}\big),

where
\alpha_{1},\beta_{1}
are inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous functions of types
(n-1)\times(n-2)
and
(m-1)\times(m-2)
respectively, and all minors on the first columns of
\tilde{V}_{1},\tilde{W}_{1}^{T}
are in
H^{\infty}.
Furthermore, the set of all level
1
superoptimal functions
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\{G-Q:Q\in\Omega_{1}\}
satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{1}=W_{0}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0\\
0&0&(F_{2}+H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)}))\cap B(t_{1})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}V_{0}^{*},

(9.98)

for some
F_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
, for a quasi-continuous unimodular function
u_{1}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{1}}{h_{1}}
, and for the closed ball
B(t_{1})
of radius
t_{1}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)})
.

Consider some
Q_{3}\in\Omega_{1},
so that, according to equation (
9.98
),

\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{1}\end{pmatrix}W_{0}(G-Q_{3})V_{0}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0\\
0&0&F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2}\end{pmatrix},

for some
\tilde{Q}_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-2)\times(n-2)}),
that is,

\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\\
\beta_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\beta_{0}^{*}\end{pmatrix}(G-Q_{3})\big(\begin{matrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{matrix}\big)\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0\\
0&0&F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

(9.99)

Observe

\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\beta_{0}^{*}\end{pmatrix}(G-Q_{3})\big(\begin{matrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{matrix}\big)=\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{pmatrix},

hence

\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\\
\beta_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0\\
0&\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

is equal to

\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\\
0&\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{pmatrix},

and so
equation (
9.99
) yields

\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\\
0&\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0\\
0&0&F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2}\end{pmatrix},

which is equivalent to the following equations

\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}=t_{1}u_{1},

\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}=0,

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}=0,

and

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}=F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2}.

(9.100)

By Theorem
4.11
applied to
H_{F_{2}},
if
(\hat{x}_{2},\hat{y}_{2})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{2}}
corresponding to
t_{2}=\|H_{F_{2}}\|,
then, for any
\tilde{Q}_{2}
which is at minimal distance from
F_{2},
we have

(F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2})\hat{x}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{y}_{2},\quad(F_{2}-\tilde{Q}_{2})^{*}\hat{y}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.

(9.101)

By equations (
9.100
) and (
9.101
),

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{y}_{2}

(9.102)

and

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}(G-Q_{3})^{*}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\hat{y}_{2}=t_{2}\hat{x}_{2}.

(9.103)

Recall that, by equations (
9.82
) and (
9.86
),

\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}\hat{x}_{2}=x_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{y}_{2}=\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2}.

(9.104)

Hence, by equation (
9.102
), we obtain

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(G-Q_{3})x_{2}=t_{2}\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}y_{2},

or equivalently,

\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}\bigg((G-Q_{3})x_{2}-t_{2}y_{2}\bigg)=0.

Since, by Theorem
9.26
,
M_{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}
is unitary, the latter equation yields

(G-Q_{3})x_{2}=t_{2}y_{2}.

Moreover, in view of equations (
9.100
), (
9.101
) and (
9.104
), equation (
9.103
) implies

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}(G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{2},

which in turn is equivalent to the equation

\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}\bigg((G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{2}-t_{2}x_{2}\bigg)=0.

By Theorem
9.26
,
M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\alpha}_{1}}
is unitary, hence the latter equation yields

(G-Q_{3})^{*}y_{2}=t_{2}x_{2}

and therefore the assertion has been proved. ∎

10.
Compactness of the operator
T_{j+1}

At this point, the reader has seen the proof of the compactness of the operators
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
.
Suppose we have applied steps
0,\dots,j
of the superoptimal analytic approximation algorithm from Subsection
4.2
to
G
, we have constructed

\begin{array}[]{lll}&t_{0}\geq t_{1}\geq\cdots\geq t_{j}>0\\
&x_{0},x_{1},\cdots,x_{j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})\\
&y_{0},y_{1},\cdots,y_{j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})\\
&h_{0},h_{1},\cdots,h_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})\;\text{outer}\\
&\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{j}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})\;\text{pointwise orthonormal on}\;\mathbb{T}\\
&\eta_{0},\eta_{1},\cdots,\eta_{j}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})\;\text{pointwise orthonormal on}\;\mathbb{T}\\
&X_{0}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}),X_{1},\cdots,X_{j}\\
&Y_{0}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{j}\\
&T_{0},T_{1},\cdots,T_{j}\;\text{compact operators},\end{array}

and all the claimed properties hold.
We shall apply a similar method to show that the operator
T_{j+1}
as given in equation (
4.37
) is compact.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
m,n
be positive integers such that
\min(m,n)\geq 2.
Let
G\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n}).
In line with the algorithm from Subsection
4.2
, let
Q_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
satisfy

(G-Q_{j})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},\quad(G-Q_{j})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1,\dots,j-1.

(10.1)

Let the spaces
X_{j},Y_{j}
be given by

X_{j}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}),\quad Y_{j}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},

and consider the compact operator

T_{j}\colon X_{j}\to Y_{j}
given by

T_{j}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}x)=P_{Y_{j}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{j})x)

for all
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).

Let
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}v_{j},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}w_{j})
be a Schmidt pair for the operator
T_{j}
corresponding to
t_{j}=\|T_{j}\|,
let
h_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
be the scalar outer factor of

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\xi_{1}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}v_{j}
,
let

x_{j}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}\dots-\xi_{j-1}\xi_{j-1}^{*})v_{j},\quad y_{j}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\dots-\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\eta_{j-1}^{T})w_{j}

and let

\xi_{j}=\frac{x_{j}}{h_{j}},\quad\eta_{j}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{y}_{j}}{h_{j}}.

(10.2)

Let, for
i=0,1,\dots,j-1
,

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.3)

be unitary-valued functions,
as described in Lemma
4.12
(see also Proposition
9.30
for

\tilde{V}_{2}
and
\tilde{W}_{2}^{T}
),

u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
are quasi-continuous unimodular functions, and

V_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad W_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{i}\end{pmatrix}.

There exist unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{j},\tilde{W}_{j}
of the form

\tilde{V}_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j}&\bar{\alpha}_{j}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{j}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}&\bar{\beta}_{j}\end{pmatrix},

(10.4)

where
\alpha_{0},\dots,\alpha_{j-1}
and
\beta_{0},\dots,\beta_{j-1}
are of types
n\times(n-1),\dots,(n-j-1)\times(n-j-2)
and
m\times(m-1),\dots,(m-j-1)\times(m-j-2)
respectively, and are inner, co-outer and quasi-continuous.

Furthermore, the set of all level
j
superoptimal error functions
\mathcal{E}_{j}
satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{j}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\cdots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&\dots&\dots&(F_{j+1}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j})\end{pmatrix}V_{j}^{*}\cdots V_{0}^{*},

(10.5)

for some
F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)}),
for the quasi-continuous unimodular functions
u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
, for all
i=0,\dots,j,
for the closed ball
B(t_{j})
of radius
t_{j}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
, and for the unitary valued functions

V_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j}\end{pmatrix},\quad W_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

Proof.

Suppose we have applied steps
0,\dots,j
of the algorithm from Subsection
4.2
and that the following diagram commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j-1}}}&\mathcal{K}_{j}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{j}\\
\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{j}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{j}$\hss}&&\hskip 12.91663pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{j}$\hss}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j-1}}}&\mathcal{L}_{j}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{j},\end{array}

(10.6)

where the maps

M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j-1}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})\to\mathcal{K}_{j}\colon x\mapsto\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j-1}x,

M_{\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j-1}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}\to\mathcal{L}_{j}\colon y\mapsto\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j-1}y,

(\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j}\to X_{j}\;\text{ and}\;(\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j}\to Y_{j}

are unitaries.

Let
(\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}v_{j},\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}w_{j})
be a Schmidt pair for the compact operator
T_{j}.
Then

x_{j}\in\mathcal{K}_{j},

y_{j}\in\mathcal{L}_{j}
are such that
(x_{j},y_{j})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j}
corresponding to
t_{j}=\|\Gamma_{j}\|,
and
(\hat{x}_{j},\hat{y}_{j})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j}}
corresponding to
t_{j}=\|H_{F_{j}}\|,
where

\hat{x}_{j}=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{j},\quad\hat{y}_{j}=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}y_{j}.

(10.7)

We intend to apply Lemma
4.12
to
H_{F_{j}}
and the Schmidt pair
(\hat{x}_{j},\hat{y}_{j})
to find unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{j},\tilde{W}_{j}
such that, for every
\tilde{Q}_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})
which is at minimal distance from
F_{j},
a factorisation of the form

F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j}=\tilde{W}_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0&F_{j+1}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{j}^{*}

is obtained, for some

F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)}).

For this purpose we find the inner-outer factorisations of
\hat{x}_{j}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j}.

By the inductive hypothesis (see Lemma
9.29
for
j=2
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}|h_{j}(z)|&=\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{j-1}(z)\wedge v_{j}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+1}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\dots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j-1}(z)\wedge w_{j}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+1}\mathbb{C}^{m}},\\
\|\hat{x}_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j}}&=\|\hat{y}_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j}}=|h_{j}(z)|,\;\text{and}\\
\|x_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&=\|y_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{j}(z)|,\end{array}

(10.8)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Equations (
10.8
) imply that
h_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
is the scalar outer factor of both
\hat{x}_{j}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j}.

By Lemma
4.12
,
\hat{x}_{j},\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j}
admit the inner-outer factorisations

\hat{x}_{j}=\hat{\xi}_{j}h_{j},\quad\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j}=\hat{\eta}_{j}h_{j},

(10.9)

where
\hat{\xi}_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})
and
\hat{\eta}_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})
are vector-valued inner functions.

By equations (
10.7
) and (
10.9
), we deduce that

\hat{\xi}_{j}=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j},\quad\hat{\eta}_{j}=\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}.

We shall show that
\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j},\;\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}
are inner in order to apply Lemma
4.12
and obtain
\tilde{V}_{j}
and
\tilde{W}_{j}
as required. We have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hat{x}_{j}&=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{j}\\
&=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\dots-\xi_{j-1}\xi_{j-1}^{*})v_{j}\\
&=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{j}-\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}v_{j}-\cdots-\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j-1}\xi_{j-1}^{*}v_{j}.\end{array}

Recall that, by the inductive hypothesis, for
i=0,\dots,j-1,
each

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

is unitary-valued, and so
\alpha_{i}^{T}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}=0
. Hence, if
0\leq i\leq j-1
, we have

\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{i+1}^{T}\alpha_{i}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}=0.

Thus

\hat{x}_{j}=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{j}=\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{j},

that is,
\hat{x}_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})
and

\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j}=\frac{1}{h_{j}}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}x_{j}=\frac{1}{h_{j}}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}v_{j}

is analytic. Moreover, by equations (
10.8
),

\|\alpha_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)x_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j}}=\|\alpha_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)v_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j}}=|h_{j}(z)|

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and hence

\|\alpha_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}(z)\xi_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j}}=1

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Therefore
\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j}
is inner.

Furthermore

\begin{array}[]{llll}\hat{y}_{j}&=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}y_{j}\\
&=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\dots-\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\eta_{j-1}^{T})w_{j}\\
&=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}w_{j}-\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}w_{j}-\cdots-\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\eta_{j-1}^{T}w_{j}.\end{array}

Notice that, by the inductive hypothesis, for
i=0,\dots,j-1,
each

\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

is unitary-valued, and so
\beta_{i}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{i}=0.

Hence, if
0\leq i\leq j-1
, we have

\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{i+1}^{*}\beta_{i}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{i}=0.

Thus

\hat{y}_{j}=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}y_{j}=\beta_{j-1}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}w_{j},

that is,

\hat{y}_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}
and

\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}=\frac{1}{h_{j}}\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{z}\bar{y}_{j}=\frac{1}{h_{j}}\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\bar{z}\bar{w}_{j}

is analytic. Further, by equations (
10.8
),

\|\beta_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\bar{z}\bar{y}_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j}}=\|\beta_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\bar{z}\bar{w}_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j}}=|h_{j}(z)|

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and therefore

\|\beta_{j-1}^{T}(z)\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}(z)\eta_{j}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=1

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
that is,
\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}
is inner.

We apply Lemma
4.12
to the Hankel operator
H_{F_{j}}
and the Schmidt pair

(\hat{x}_{j},\hat{y}_{j})
to deduce that there exist inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous functions
\alpha_{j},\beta_{j}
of types
(n-j)\times(n-j-1),

(m-j)\times(m-j-1)
respectively such that

\tilde{V}_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{j}&\bar{\alpha}_{j}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{j}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{j-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{j}&\bar{\beta}_{j}\end{pmatrix}

are unitary-valued and all minors on the first columns of
\tilde{V}_{j},\tilde{W}_{j}
are in
H^{\infty}.
Moreover, every function
\hat{Q}_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}),
which is at minimal distance from
F_{j},
satisfies

F_{j}-\hat{Q}_{j}=\tilde{W}_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0&F_{j+1}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{j}^{*},

for some
F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
and for the quasi-continuous unimodular function
u_{j}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{j}}{h_{j}}.

By Lemma
4.15
, the set

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{j}=\{F_{j}-\hat{Q}:\hat{Q}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}),\|F_{j}-\hat{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}=t_{j}\}

satisfies

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{j}=\tilde{W}_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0&(F_{j+1}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j})\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{j}^{*},

where
B(t_{j})
is the closed ball of radius
t_{j}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)}).

By the inductive hypothesis, the set of all level
j
superoptimal error functions
\mathcal{E}_{j}
satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{j-1}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j-1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\cdots&0&0_{1\times(n-j)}\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0&0_{1\times(n-j)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&t_{j-1}u_{j-1}&0\\
0_{(m-j)\times 1}&0_{(m-j)\times 1}&\dots&\dots&(F_{j}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j-1})\end{pmatrix}V_{j-1}^{*}\cdots V_{0}^{*},

(10.10)

for some
F_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}),

u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
are quasi-continuous unimodular functions for all
i=0,\dots,j-1
, and for the closed ball
B(t_{j-1})
of radius
t_{j-1}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}).

Thus, by equation (
10.10
),
\mathcal{E}_{j}
admits the factorisation (
10.5
) as claimed. ∎

Remark 10.2
.

Let, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.11)

be unitary-valued functions,
as described in Lemma
4.12
. Let

V_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j}\end{pmatrix},\quad W_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
A_{j}=\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{j}
,
A_{-1}=I_{n}
,
B_{j}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\dots\beta_{j}
and
B_{-1}=I_{m}
.

Note

W_{1}W_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\eta_{1}^{T}\beta_{0}\\
0&\beta_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\beta_{0}^{*}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\\
B_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}

and

W_{2}W_{1}W_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{2}&0\\
0&\eta_{2}^{T}B_{1}\\
0&\beta_{2}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\\
B_{1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\\
\eta_{2}^{T}B_{1}B_{1}^{*}\\
B_{2}^{*}\end{pmatrix}.

Similarly one obtains

W_{j}W_{j-1}\cdots W_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{0}^{T}\\
\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\eta_{j}^{T}B_{j-1}B_{j-1}^{*}\\
B_{j}^{*}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.12)

Therefore

W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{0}&B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}&\dots&B_{j-1}B_{j-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{j}&B_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.13)

Thus

I_{m}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}W_{j}\cdots W_{1}W_{0}=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}B_{i-1}B_{i-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}B_{i-1}B_{i-1}^{*}+B_{j}B_{j}^{*}.

(10.14)

Furthermore

V_{0}V_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{\alpha}_{0}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{\alpha}_{1}\par\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{A}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

and

V_{0}V_{1}V_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{A}_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I_{2}&0&0\\
0&A_{1}^{T}\xi_{2}&\bar{\alpha}_{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{A}_{1}A_{1}^{T}\xi_{2}&\bar{A}_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

One can easily show by induction that

V_{0}\cdots V_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{A}_{1}A_{1}^{T}\xi_{2}&\dots&\bar{A}_{j-1}A_{j-1}^{T}\xi_{j}&\bar{A}_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.15)

Therefore,

I_{n}=V_{0}\cdots V_{j}V_{j}^{*}\cdots V_{0}^{*}=\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}+\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}+\dots\bar{A}_{j-1}A_{j-1}^{T}\xi_{j}\xi_{j}^{*}\bar{A}_{j-1}A_{j-1}^{T}+\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}.

(10.16)

Lemma 10.3
.

Let

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.17)

be unitary-valued functions, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,
as described in Lemma
4.12

For
i=0,1,\dots,j
, let
A_{i}=\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{i}
and
A_{-1}=I_{n}
.
Then, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,

\bar{A}_{i}A_{i}^{T}=I_{n}-\sum\limits_{k=0}^{i}\xi_{k}\xi_{k}^{*}

(10.18)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
.

Proof.

By equation (
10.16
), for
k=0,\dots,j
,

\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}=I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\bar{A}_{i-1}A_{i-1}^{T}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*}\bar{A}_{i-1}A_{i-1}^{T}.

(10.19)

Thus to prove condition (
10.18
) it suffices to show that, for
k=0,\dots,j,

\bar{A}_{k-1}A_{k-1}^{T}\xi_{k}\xi_{k}^{*}\bar{A}_{k-1}A_{k-1}^{T}=\xi_{k}\xi_{k}^{*}.

For
k=0,

\bar{A}_{-1}A_{-1}^{T}\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}\bar{A}_{-1}A_{-1}^{T}=\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*},

and so, equation (
10.19
) yields

\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}=I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}.

For
k=1,

\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*})\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*})

By Proposition
5.1
,
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{0}
are pointwise orthogonal almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
hence

\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}=\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*},

and in view of equation (
10.19
), we get

\bar{A}_{1}A_{1}^{T}=I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\xi_{1}\xi_{1}^{*}.

Suppose

\bar{A}_{\ell-1}A_{\ell-1}^{T}\xi_{\ell}\xi_{\ell}^{*}\bar{A}_{\ell-1}A_{\ell-1}^{T}=\xi_{\ell}\xi_{\ell}^{*}

(10.20)

holds for every
\ell\leq k
, where
0\leq k\leq j
, almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
. By equations (
10.19
) and (
10.20
), this implies

\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}=I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*}.

Let us show that

\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}=\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}.

Note that

\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}=(I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*})\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}(I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*}).

By Proposition
5.1
, the set
\{\xi_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{k+1}
is pointwise orthogonal almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and therefore

\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}\bar{A}_{k}A_{k}^{T}=\xi_{k+1}\xi_{k+1}^{*}.

Thus, by equation (
10.19
),

\bar{A}_{k+1}A_{k+1}^{T}=I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k+1}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
, and the assertion has been proved. ∎

Lemma 10.4
.

Let

\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.21)

be unitary-valued functions, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,
as described in Lemma
4.12
.
For
i=0,1,\dots,j
, let
B_{i}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\dots\beta_{i}
and
B_{-1}=I_{m}
. Then, for
k=0,1,\dots,j
,

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}

(10.22)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
.

Proof.

By equation (
10.14
), for
k=0,\dots,j
,

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}B_{i-1}B_{i-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}B_{i-1}B_{i-1}^{*}.

(10.23)

Thus to prove condition (
10.22
) it suffices to show that, for
k=0,\dots,j,

B_{k-1}B_{k-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{k}\eta_{k}^{T}B_{k-1}B_{k-1}^{*}=\bar{\eta}_{k}\eta_{k}^{T}.

For
k=0,

B_{-1}B_{-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}B_{-1}B_{-1}^{*}=I_{m}\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}I_{m}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T},

and so, equation (
10.23
) yields

B_{0}B_{0}^{*}=I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}.

For
k=1,

B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T})\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}).

By Proposition
5.1
,
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{0}
are pointwise orthogonal almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
hence

B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}=\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T},

and in view of equation (
10.23
), we get

B_{1}B_{1}^{*}=I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\bar{\eta}_{1}\eta_{1}^{T}.

Suppose

B_{\ell-1}B_{\ell-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{\ell}\eta_{\ell}^{T}B_{\ell-1}B_{\ell-1}^{*}=\bar{\eta}_{\ell}\eta_{\ell}^{T}

(10.24)

holds for every
\ell\leq k
, where
0\leq k\leq j
, almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
. By equations (
10.23
) and
(
10.24
), this implies

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}.

Let us show that

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}.

Note that

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T})\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}).

By Proposition
5.1
, the set
\{\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{k+1}
is pointwise orthogonal almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and therefore

B_{k}B_{k}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}B_{k}B_{k}^{*}=\bar{\eta}_{k+1}\eta_{k+1}^{T}.

Thus, by equation (
10.23
),

B_{k+1}B_{k+1}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k+1}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
, and the assertion has been proved. ∎

The following statement asserts that any function
Q_{j+1}\in\Omega_{j}
necessarily satisfies equations (
4.34
).

Proposition 10.5
.

Every
Q_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
which minimises

(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q),\dots,s_{j}^{\infty}(G-Q))

satisfies

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i},\quad\text{for}\;i=0,1,\dots,j.

Proof.

By the recursive step of the algorithm from Subsection
4.2
, every
Q_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
that minimises

(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q),\dots,s_{j-1}^{\infty}(G-Q))

satisfies

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},\quad(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1,\dots,j-1.

Hence it suffices to show that
Q_{j+1}
satisfies

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{j}=t_{j}y_{j},(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{j}=t_{j}x_{j}.

Notice that, by the inductive step, the following diagram commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j-1}}}&\mathcal{K}_{j}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{j}\\
\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{j}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{j}$\hss}&&\hskip 12.91663pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{j}$\hss}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j-1}}}&\mathcal{L}_{j}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{j},\end{array}

(10.25)

where the maps
M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j-1}},

M_{\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j-1}},

(\xi_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j}\to X_{j}
and
(\bar{\eta}_{(j-1)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j}\to Y_{j}
are unitaries, and
F_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}).

By equation (
10.10
), the set of all level
j-1
superoptimal error functions

\mathcal{E}_{j-1}=\{G-Q:Q\in\Omega_{j-1}\}

satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{j-1}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j-1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\cdots&0&0_{1\times(n-j)}\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0&0_{1\times(n-j)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&t_{j-1}u_{j-1}&0\\
0_{(m-j)\times 1}&0_{(m-j)\times 1}&\dots&\dots&(F_{j}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j-1})\end{pmatrix}V_{j-1}^{*}\cdots V_{0}^{*},

(10.26)

for some
F_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}),
for quasi-continuous unimodular functions
u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
,
i=0,\dots,j-1,
and the closed ball
B(t_{j-1})
of radius
t_{j-1}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)}).

Consider some
Q_{j+1}\in\Omega_{j-1},
so that, according to equation (
10.26
),

\footnotesize{\begin{pmatrix}I_{j-1}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j-1}\end{pmatrix}\cdots W_{0}(G-Q_{j+1})V_{0}\cdots\begin{pmatrix}I_{n-j-1}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j-1}\end{pmatrix}=\scriptsize{\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\dots&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0\\
\vdots&\hskip 30.13885pt\ddots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&t_{j-1}u_{j-1}&0\\
0&\dots&\dots&F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j}\end{pmatrix}}},

(10.27)

where
\tilde{Q}_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})
is at minimal distance from
F_{j}.
Let
B_{j}=\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}
and let
A_{j}=\alpha_{0}\cdots\alpha_{j}
.

By equations (
10.3
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\begin{pmatrix}I_{j-1}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j-1}\end{pmatrix}\cdots W_{0}(G-Q_{j+1})V_{0}\cdots\begin{pmatrix}I_{n-j-1}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j-1}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=\footnotesize{\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\dots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\dots&\vdots\\
0&\dots&\eta_{j-1}^{T}B_{j-2}B_{j-2}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-2}A_{j-2}^{T}\xi_{j-1}&\eta_{j-1}^{T}B_{j-2}B_{j-2}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-1}\\
0&\dots&B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-2}A_{j-2}^{T}\xi_{j-1}&B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-1}\end{pmatrix}},\end{array}

which, combined with equation (
10.27
), yields

B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-1}=F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j}.

(10.28)

Since
\tilde{Q}_{j}
is at minimal distance from
F_{j},

\|F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j}\|_{\infty}=\|H_{F_{j}}\|=t_{j}.

Note that, if
(\hat{x}_{j},\hat{y}_{j})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j}}
corresponding to
t_{j},
then, by Theorem
4.11
,

(F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j})\hat{x}_{j}=t_{j}\hat{y}_{j},\quad(F_{j}-\tilde{Q}_{j})^{*}\hat{y}_{j}=t_{j}\hat{x}_{j}.

In view of equation (
10.28
), the latter equations imply

B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-1}\hat{x}_{j}=t_{j}\hat{y}_{j},\quad A_{j-1}^{T}(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}B_{j-1}\hat{y}_{j}=t_{j}\hat{x}_{j}.

By equation (
10.7
),

\hat{x}_{j}=A_{j-1}^{T}x_{j},\quad\hat{y}_{j}=B_{j-1}^{*}y_{j}

Thus

B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j-1}\hat{x}_{j}=B_{j-1}^{*}(G-Q_{j+1})x_{j}=t_{j}B_{j-1}^{*}y_{j},

or equivalently,

B_{j-1}^{*}\big((G-Q_{j+1})x_{j}-t_{j}y_{j}\big)=0,

and since, by the inductive hypothesis,
M_{B_{j-1}}
is a unitary map, we have

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{j}=t_{j}y_{j}.

Furthermore

A_{j-1}^{T}(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}B_{j-1}\hat{y}_{j}=A_{j-1}^{T}(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{j}=t_{j}A_{j-1}^{T}x_{j},

or equivalently,

A_{j-1}^{T}\big((G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{j}-t_{j}x_{j}\big)=0.

By the inductive hypothesis,
M_{\bar{A}_{j-1}}
is a unitary map, hence

(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{j}=t_{j}x_{j},

and therefore
Q_{j+1}
satisfies the required equations. ∎

Lemma 10.6
.

Let

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\;\;i=0,1,\dots,j,

(10.29)

be unitary-valued functions,
as described in Lemma
4.12
. Then

\alpha_{l}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-l})=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-l-1})

and

\beta_{l}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-l})^{\perp}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-l-1})^{\perp},

for all
l=0,\dots,j.

Proof.

Recall that, by Lemma
4.18
, for all
l=0,\dots,j,
the inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous functions
\alpha_{l},\beta_{l}
of types
(n-l)\times(n-l-1)
and
(m-l)\times(m-l-1)
respectively, are left invertible. The rest of the proof is similar to
Lemmas
9.4
and
9.9
. ∎

As a preparation for proof of the main inductive step we prove several propositions.

Proposition 10.7
.

Let

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.30)

be unitary-valued functions, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,
as described in Lemma
4.12

Let
A_{i}=\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{i}
, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
, and
A_{-1}=I_{n}
. Let

V_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\;\text{for}\;i=0,1,\dots,j,

and let

\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=V_{0}\cdots V_{j}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})\end{pmatrix}.

(10.31)

Let
\xi_{(j)}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}.
Then,

\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})

and the operator
(\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
is unitary.

Proof.

First let us prove that

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).

By equations (
10.31
) and (
10.15
)

\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=\bar{A}_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}).

(10.32)

By Lemma
10.6
,

\alpha_{l}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-l})=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-l-1})

for all
l=0,\dots,j.
Thus

\begin{array}[]{lll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})&=\alpha_{j}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})\\
&=\alpha_{j}^{T}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j+1})\\
&=\alpha_{j}^{T}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\alpha_{j-2}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j+2})\\
&=\dots\\
&=\alpha_{j}^{T}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\dots\alpha_{1}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-1})\\
&=\alpha_{j}^{T}\alpha_{j-1}^{T}\dots\alpha_{1}^{T}\alpha_{0}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})\\
&=A_{j}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).\end{array}

(10.33)

By equations (
10.32
) and (
10.33
),

\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=\bar{A}_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})=\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).

(10.34)

By Lemma
10.3
,

\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}=I_{n}-\sum\limits_{k=0}^{j}\xi_{k}\xi_{k}^{*}.

(10.35)

By Proposition
5.1
,
\{\xi_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{j}
is an orthonormal set in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T}.

Therefore, by equations (
10.34
) and (
10.35
),

\begin{array}[]{lll}\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}&=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})\\
&=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}(I_{n}-\sum\limits_{k=0}^{j}\xi_{k}\xi_{k}^{*})H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})\\
&=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).\end{array}

(10.36)

To show that the operator
(\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
is unitary, it suffices to prove that, for every
\vartheta\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1},

\|\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\vartheta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n})}=\|\vartheta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})}.

Let
\vartheta\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1}.
Then, by Proposition
3.12
, we have

\begin{array}[]{llll}\|\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\vartheta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n})}^{2}&=\langle\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\vartheta,\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\vartheta\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n})}\\
&=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\xi_{(j)}(e^{i\theta})\dot{\wedge}\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\xi_{(j)}(e^{i\theta})\dot{\wedge}\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}}d\theta\\
&=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&\dots&\langle\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
\langle\xi_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&\dots&\langle\xi_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&\dots&\langle\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta.\end{array}

By Proposition
5.1
,
\{\xi_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{j}
is an orthonormal set in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T}.

Thus the latter integral is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&\langle\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
0&1&\dots&\langle\xi_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
\vdots&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\xi_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&~&\dots&\langle\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta.

Note that since
\vartheta\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1},

\vartheta=\bar{A}_{l}A_{j}^{T}\psi=(I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*})\psi

for some
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}).
Then, for almost every
e^{i\theta}\in\mathbb{T},

\begin{array}[]{lll}\langle\xi_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&=\langle\xi_{k}(e^{i\theta}),(I_{n}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{*})(e^{i\theta})\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
&=\langle\xi_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}-\langle\xi_{k},\xi_{k}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\langle\xi_{k},\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=0.\end{array}

Hence

\|\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\vartheta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n})}^{2}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&~&0&\dots&0\\
0&~&1&\dots&0\\
\vdots&~&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&~&~&\dots&\langle\vartheta(e^{i\theta}),\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta,

which yields

\begin{array}[]{clll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\|\vartheta(e^{i\theta})\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}^{2}d\theta=\|\vartheta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})}^{2}.\end{array}

Therefore, the operator
(\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
is unitary. ∎

Proposition 10.8
.

Let

\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.37)

be unitary-valued functions, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
,
as described in Lemma
4.12
.
Let
B_{i}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\dots\beta_{i}
, for
i=0,1,\dots,j
, and
B_{-1}=I_{m}
.
Let
W_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}\end{pmatrix},\;\text{for}\;i=0,1,\dots,j,
and let

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=W_{0}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}\end{pmatrix},

(10.38)

Let
\bar{\eta}_{(j)}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}.

Then,

\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}

and the operator
(\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j+1}\to\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
is unitary.

Proof.

First let us prove that

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.

By equations (
10.38
) and (
10.13
),

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=B_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}.

(10.39)

By Lemma
10.6
,

\beta_{\ell}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-\ell})^{\perp}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-\ell-1})^{\perp},

for all
\ell=0,\dots,j.
Thus

\begin{array}[]{lll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}&=\beta_{j}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}\\
&=\beta_{j}^{*}\beta_{j-1}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j+1})^{\perp}\\
&=\beta_{j}^{*}\beta_{j-1}^{*}\beta_{j-2}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j+2})^{\perp}\\
&=\dots\\
&=\beta_{j}^{*}\beta_{j-1}^{*}\dots\beta_{1}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-1})^{\perp}\\
&=\beta_{j}^{*}\beta_{j-1}^{*}\dots\beta_{1}^{*}\beta_{0}^{*}(H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}\\
&=B_{j}^{*}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.\end{array}

(10.40)

By equations (
10.39
) and (
10.40
),

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=B_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}=B_{j}B_{j}^{*}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.

(10.41)

By Lemma
10.4
,

B_{j}B_{j}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}.

(10.42)

Thus

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T})H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.

(10.43)

By Proposition
5.1
,
\{\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{j}
is an orthonormal set in
\mathbb{C}^{m}
for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T}.

Therefore, by equations (
10.41
) and (
10.43
),

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\mathcal{L}_{j+1}&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T})H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}\\
&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.\end{array}

(10.44)

To show that the operator
(\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j+1}\to\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
is unitary, it suffices to prove that, for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1},

\|\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}=\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

By Proposition
3.12
, we have

\begin{array}[]{clllll}\|\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}^{2}&=\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\varphi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}\\
&=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta.\end{array}

By Proposition
5.1
, the set
\{\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\}_{i=0}^{j}
is orthonormal in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T}.

Then the latter integral is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
0&1&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\vdots&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\dots&\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta.

Note that since
\varphi\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1},

\varphi=(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T})\psi,

for some
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
Then, for almost every

e^{i\theta}\in\mathbb{T},

\begin{array}[]{lll}\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&=\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),(I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T})(e^{i\theta})\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&=\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}-\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=0.\end{array}

Thus

\begin{array}[]{clll}\|\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}^{2}=&\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&0\\
0&1&\dots&0\\
\vdots&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\dots&\dots&\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta\\
&=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\varphi(e^{i\theta}),\varphi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}d\theta=\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})}^{2}.\end{array}

Hence the operator
(\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j+1}\to\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
is unitary.∎

Proposition 10.9
.

With the notation of Proposition
10.8

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}=\{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m}):\beta_{j}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})\}.

Proof.

Clearly
\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}.

The general element of
\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}
is
\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}\bar{z}\bar{g}
with
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})
. A function
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})
belongs to
\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}

if and only if

\langle f,\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}\bar{z}\bar{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})

if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle f(e^{i\theta}),\beta_{0}(e^{i\theta})\cdots\beta_{j}(e^{i\theta})e^{-i\theta}\bar{g}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}d\theta=0\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})

if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\beta_{j}^{*}(e^{i\theta})\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}(e^{i\theta})f(e^{i\theta}),e^{-i\theta}\bar{g}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m-2}}d\theta=0\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}).

The latter statement is equivalent to the assertion that

\beta_{j}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}f
is orthogonal to
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}),
which holds if and only if
