\beta_{j}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}).

Thus

\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}=\{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m}):\beta_{j}^{*}\cdots\beta_{0}^{*}f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})\}

as required. ∎

Let us proceed to the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 10.10
.

Let
m,n
be positive integers such that
\min(m,n)\geq 2.
Let
G
be in
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
. In the notation of the algorithm
4.2
, let

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}v_{j},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}w_{j})

be a Schmidt pair for
T_{j}
corresponding to
t_{j}=\|T_{j}\|\neq 0.
Let

h_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
be the scalar outer factor of

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j-1}\dot{\wedge}v_{j}.

Let

x_{j}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\dots-\xi_{j-1}\xi_{j-1}^{*})v_{j},

y_{j}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\dots-\bar{\eta}_{j-1}\eta_{j-1}^{T})w_{j}

and

\xi_{j}=\frac{x_{j}}{h_{j}},\quad\bar{\eta}_{j}=\frac{zy_{j}}{\bar{h}_{j}}.

For
i=0,1,\dots,j
, let

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

(10.45)

be unitary-valued functions,
as described in Lemma
4.12
. Let

V_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j}\end{pmatrix},\quad W_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{j}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
A_{j}=\alpha_{0}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{j}
,
A_{-1}=I_{n}
,
B_{j}=\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\dots\beta_{j}
and
B_{-1}=I_{m}
.
Let

X_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})\subset H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\wedge^{j+2}{\mathbb{C}^{n}}),

and let

Y_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}\subset H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.

Let

T_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x)=P_{Y_{j+1}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{j+1})x)

for all
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
, where
Q_{j+1}
satisfies

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},\;\text{and}\;\;(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i},\quad\text{for}\;i=0,1,\dots,j.

(10.46)

Let

\mathcal{K}_{j+1}=V_{0}\cdots V_{j}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathcal{L}_{j+1}=W_{0}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.47)

Let the operator
\Gamma_{j+1}\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
be given by

\Gamma_{j+1}=P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}M_{G-Q_{j+1}}|_{\mathcal{K}_{j+1}}.

Then

(i)

The maps

M_{\bar{A}_{j}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})\to\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\colon x\mapsto\bar{A}_{j}x,\;\;\text{and}\;\;M_{B_{j}}\colon H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}\to\mathcal{L}_{j+1}\colon y\mapsto{B_{j}}y

are unitaries.

(ii)

The maps
(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to X_{j+1},

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\colon\mathcal{L}_{j+1}\to Y_{j+1}
are unitaries.

(iii)

the following diagram commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\cdots\bar{\alpha}_{j}}}&\mathcal{K}_{j+1}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{j+1}\\
\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{j+1}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{j+1}$\hss}&&\hskip 12.91663pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{j+1}$\hss}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{\beta_{0}\cdots\beta_{j}}}&\mathcal{L}_{j+1}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{j+1},\end{array}

(10.48)

where
F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
is the function defined in Proposition
10.1
;

(iv)

{\Gamma}_{j+1}
and
T_{j+1}
are compact operators;

(v)

\|T_{j+1}\|=\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|=\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|=t_{j+1}.

Proof.

(i) It follows from Lemma
4.16
.
(ii) follows from Propositions
10.7
and
10.8
. (iii)
By Theorem
1.3
, there exists a function
Q_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
such that
the sequence

\left(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),\dots,s_{j+1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1})\right)

is lexicographically minimized.
By Proposition
10.5
, any such
Q_{j+1}
satisfies

(G-Q_{j+1})x_{i}=t_{i}y_{i},(G-Q_{j+1})^{*}y_{i}=t_{i}x_{i},\quad\text{for}\;i=0,1,\dots,j.

(10.49)

By Proposition
8.1
,
T_{j+1}
is well-defined and is independent of the choice of
Q_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
satisfying equations
(
10.49
). We can choose
Q_{j+1}
which minimises

\left(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),\dots,s_{j+1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1})\right),

and therefore satisfies equations
(
10.49
). Consider the following diagram.

\begin{array}[]{clllll}&\mathcal{K}_{j+1}&\xrightarrow{\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})=X_{j+1}\\
&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\Gamma_{j+1}$\hss}&&\hskip 55.97205pt\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle T_{j+1}$\hss}\\
&\mathcal{L}_{j+1}&\xrightarrow{\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{{\eta}}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot}&\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}=Y_{j+1}.\end{array}

(10.50)

Let us prove first that diagram (
10.50
) commutes.
By Proposition
10.1
, every
Q_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})
, which minimises

\left(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1}),\dots,s_{j+1}^{\infty}(G-Q_{j+1})\right),

satisfies the following equation (see equation (
10.5
)).

G-Q_{j+1}=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\cdots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&\dots&\dots&(F_{j+1}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j})\end{pmatrix}V_{j}^{*}\cdots V_{0}^{*},

(10.51)

Thus, for every
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,

(G-Q_{j+1})V_{0}\cdots V_{j}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}\end{pmatrix}=\hskip 170.71652pt

(10.52)

W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\cdots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0&0_{1\times(n-j-1)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&t_{j}u_{j}&0\\
0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&0_{(m-j-1)\times 1}&\dots&\dots&(F_{j+1}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0_{(j+1)\times 1}\\
H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}\end{pmatrix},

for some
F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)}),
for the quasi-continuous unimodular functions
u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
, for all
i=0,\dots,j,
for the closed ball
B(t_{j})
of radius
t_{j}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
.
By equation (
10.13
),

W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j}^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{0}&B_{0}B_{0}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{1}&\dots&B_{j-1}B_{j-1}^{*}\bar{\eta}_{j}&B_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

By equation (
10.15
),

V_{0}\cdots V_{j}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}&\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}\xi_{1}&\bar{A}_{1}A_{1}^{T}\xi_{2}&\dots&\bar{A}_{j-1}A_{j-1}^{T}\xi_{j-1}&\bar{A}_{j}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.53)

Therefore, by equation (
10.52
), for every
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,

(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j}\chi=B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi.

(10.54)

A typical element
x\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1}
is of the form
x=\bar{A}_{j}\chi,
for some
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}).
Then, by Proposition
10.7
,

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\bar{A}_{j}\chi=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\bar{A}_{j}\chi\in X_{j+1}.

Therefore, by the definition of
T_{j+1}
and by equation (
10.54
),

\begin{array}[]{cllll}T_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\bar{A}_{j}\chi)&=P_{Y_{j+1}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{j+1})\bar{A}_{j}\chi)\\
&=P_{Y_{j+1}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi).\end{array}

Furthermore, by the definition of
\Gamma_{j+1}
and by equation (
10.54
),

\begin{array}[]{cllll}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\Gamma_{j+1}(\bar{A}_{j}\chi)&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}(G-Q_{j+1})(\bar{A}_{j}\chi)\\
&=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi.\end{array}

In order to prove the commutativity of diagram (
10.50
), we need to show that, for every
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,

T_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\bar{A}_{j}\chi)=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\Gamma_{j+1}(\bar{A}_{j}\chi).

Hence we must prove that, for every
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi\in Y_{j+1}

and that

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\left(B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi-P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi\right),\;\text{which is equal to }\;\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi,

is orthogonal to
Y_{j+1}.

Observe that, by Proposition
10.8
, for any
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,
\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi
is indeed an element of
Y_{j+1}.

To prove that

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi

is orthogonal to
Y_{j+1},

it suffices to prove that

\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\Phi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0

for
\Phi=P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi
, for all
\chi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
and
for all
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
By Proposition
3.12
,

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\Phi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}\\
&\vskip 4.30554pt=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&\dots&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta\end{array}

for all
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
Recall, by Proposition
5.1
, the set
\{\eta_{i}\}_{i=0}^{j}
is an orthonormal set in
\mathbb{C}^{m}
almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Hence

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\Phi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}\\
&\vskip 4.30554pt=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
0&1&\dots&\langle\bar{\eta}_{1}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
\vdots&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&~&\dots&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}d\theta.\end{array}

Multiplying the
k
-th column by
\langle\bar{\eta}_{k}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}
and subtracting it from the last column of the determinant above, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\scriptsize{\det\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&0\\
0&1&\dots&0\\
\vdots&~&\ddots&\vdots\\
\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{0}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}&~&\dots&\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&~&~&-\sum_{i=0}^{j}\langle\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\bar{\eta}_{i}(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\langle\bar{\eta}_{i}(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}\end{pmatrix}}d\theta,

which is equal to

\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\psi^{*}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta})&-\sum_{i=0}^{j}\psi^{*}(e^{i\theta})\bar{\eta}_{i}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{i}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta})d\theta\\
&=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\psi^{*}(e^{i\theta})\bigg(I_{m}-\sum_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{i}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\bigg)\Phi(e^{i\theta})d\theta.\end{array}

Then

\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\Phi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0

for all
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left\langle\bigg(I_{m}-\sum_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}(e^{i\theta})\eta_{i}^{T}(e^{i\theta})\bigg)\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=0

for all
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp},

which holds if and only if

\bigg(I_{m}-\sum_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}\bigg)\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m}).

By Lemma
10.4
,

B_{j}B_{j}^{*}=I_{m}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\bar{\eta}_{i}\eta_{i}^{T}.

(10.55)

Thus

\langle\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\Phi,\bar{\eta}_{(j)}\dot{\wedge}\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0

for all
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle B_{j-1}(e^{i\theta})B_{j-1}^{*}(e^{i\theta})\Phi(e^{i\theta}),\psi(e^{i\theta})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=0,

which holds if and only if
B_{j}B_{j}^{*}\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m}).

By Proposition
10.9
,
\Phi=P_{\mathcal{L}_{j+1}^{\perp}}B_{j}F_{j+1}\chi
satisfies the following property
B_{j}^{*}\Phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}).

Hence diagram (
10.50
) commutes.

Recall that, by Lemma
4.17
, the following diagram also commutes

\begin{array}[]{clllll}&H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})&\xrightarrow{M_{\bar{A}_{j}}}&\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\\
&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle H_{F_{j+1}}$\hss}&&\Big\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle{\Gamma}_{j+1}$\hss}\\
&H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1})^{\perp}&\xrightarrow{M_{B_{j}}}&\mathcal{L}_{j+1}.\end{array}

(10.56)

(iv)
Since
F_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(n-j-1)\times(m-j-1)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(n-j-1)\times(m-j-1)}),
by Hartman’s Theorem, the Hankel operator
H_{F_{j+1}}
is compact.
Since diagram (
10.56
) commutes and
the operators
M_{\bar{A}_{j}}
and
\;M_{B_{j}}
are unitaries,
{\Gamma}_{j+1}

is compact.
By (iii),

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\circ(M_{B_{j}}\circ H_{F_{j+1}}\circ M_{\bar{A}_{j}}^{*})\circ(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}=T_{j+1}.

By (i) and (ii), the operators

M_{\bar{A}_{j}},\;M_{B_{j}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries,
Hence
T_{j+1}
is a compact operator.

(v) Since diagram (
10.48
) commutes and
the operators
M_{\bar{A}_{j}},\;M_{B_{j}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\dots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries,

\|T_{j+1}\|=\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|=\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|=t_{j+1}.

∎

Lemma 10.11
.

Let
v_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n})
and
w_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}
be such that

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})

is a Schmidt pair for the operator
T_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|T_{j+1}\|.
Then

(i)

there exist
x_{j+1}\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1}
and
y_{j+1}\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
such that
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for the operator
\Gamma_{j+1}
;

(ii)

for any
x_{j+1}\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1}
and
y_{j+1}\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
such that

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1},\quad\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}y_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1},

the pair
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|.

Proof.

(i) By Theorem
10.10
, the operator
\Gamma_{j+1}\colon\mathcal{K}_{j+1}\to\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
is compact and

\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|=\|T_{j+1}\|=t_{j+1}.

Hence there exist
x_{j+1}\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1},

y_{j+1}\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
such that
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|=t_{j+1}.

(ii) Suppose that
x_{j+1}\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1},y_{j+1}\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1}
satisfy

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1},

(10.57)

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}y_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1}.

(10.58)

Let us show that
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1},
that is,

\Gamma_{j+1}x_{j+1}=t_{j+1}y_{j+1},\quad\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}x_{j+1}.

Since diagram (
10.50
) commutes,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle T_{j+1}\circ(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)\circ\Gamma_{j+1}\;

(10.59)

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}\circ T_{j+1}^{*}=\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}\circ(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}.

By hypothesis,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle T_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1})=t_{j+1}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})\;

(10.60)

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle T_{j+1}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})=t_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1}).

Thus, by equations (
10.57
), (
10.58
) and (
10.60
),

\begin{array}[]{clllll}\Gamma_{j+1}x_{j+1}&=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}T_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1})\\
&=(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}t_{j+1}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})\\
&=t_{j+1}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}y_{j+1}).\end{array}

Hence

\Gamma_{j+1}x_{j+1}=t_{j+1}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}y_{j+1}.

By equation (
10.57
),

x_{j+1}=(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1}),

and, by equation (
10.58
),

(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})=y_{j+1}.

Thus

\begin{array}[]{clll}\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}y_{j+1}&=\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})\\
&=(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\cdot)^{*}T_{j+1}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1}),\end{array}

the last equality following by the second equation of (
10.59
). By equations (
10.57
) and (
10.60
), we have

T_{j+1}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})=t_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1})=t_{j+1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x_{j+1}),

and so,

\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}x_{j+1}.

Therefore
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|=t_{j+1}.
∎

Lemma 10.12
.

Suppose that

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})

is a Schmidt pair for the operator
T_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|T_{j+1}\|=t_{j+1}.
Let

x_{j+1}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\cdots-\xi_{j}\xi_{j}^{*})v_{j+1},

y_{j+1}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\cdots-\bar{\eta}_{j}\eta_{j}^{T})w_{j+1},

and let

\hat{x}_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1},\quad\hat{y}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}.

Then

(i)
the pair
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for the operator
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
t_{j+1}
;

(ii)
the pair
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j+1}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|=t_{j+1}.

Proof.

By Lemmas
10.3
and
10.4
,

x_{j+1}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\cdots-\xi_{j}\xi_{j}^{*})v_{j+1}=\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1}

(10.61)

and

y_{j+1}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\cdots-\bar{\eta}_{j}\eta_{j}^{T})w_{j+1}=B_{j}B_{j}^{*}w_{j+1}.

(10.62)

Hence

\hat{x}_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1}

(10.63)

and

\hat{y}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}B_{j}B_{j}^{*}w_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}w_{j+1}.

(10.64)

These imply that
\hat{x}_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})
,
x_{j+1}\in\mathcal{K}_{j+1}
,
\hat{y}_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1})^{\perp}
and
y_{j+1}\in\mathcal{L}_{j+1}.

By Proposition
5.1
,

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1}\quad\text{and}\;\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}y_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1}.

Thus, by Proposition
10.11
, the pair
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
\|\Gamma_{j+1}\|.
Therefore,

\Gamma_{j+1}x_{j+1}=t_{j+1}y_{j+1},\quad\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}x_{j+1}.

(10.65)

To show that the pair
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j+1}}
corresponding to
t_{j+1}
, we need to prove that

H_{F_{j+1}}\hat{x}_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{y}_{j+1},\quad H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}\hat{y}_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{x}_{j+1}.

By Theorem
10.10
,

H_{F_{j+1}}=(M_{B_{j}})^{*}\circ\Gamma_{j+1}\circ M_{\bar{A}_{j}},

(10.66)

and

H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}=M_{\bar{A}_{j}}^{*}\circ\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}\circ M_{B_{j}}.

(10.67)

By equation (
10.66
), we have

\displaystyle H_{F_{j+1}}\hat{x}_{j+1}

\displaystyle=H_{F_{j+1}}A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=B_{j}^{*}\Gamma_{j+1}\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}.

(10.68)

Notice that, by equations (
10.61
) and (
10.63
),

x_{j+1}=\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}.

(10.69)

Hence, by equations (
10.65
) and (
10
), we obtain

H_{F_{j+1}}\hat{x}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}\Gamma_{j+1}x_{j+1}=t_{j+1}B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{y}_{j+1}.

Let us show that
H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}\hat{y}_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{x}_{j+1}
. By equations (
10.67
) and (
10.65
), we have

\displaystyle H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}\hat{y}_{j+1}

\displaystyle=H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}\vskip 8.61108pt

\displaystyle=A_{j}^{T}\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}B_{j}B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}

(10.70)

Observe that, in view of equations (
10.62
) and (
10.64
),

y_{j+1}=B_{j}B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}.

(10.71)

Hence, by equations (
10.65
) and (
10.70
), we obtain

H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}\hat{y}_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}\Gamma_{j+1}^{*}y_{j+1}=t_{j+1}A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{x}_{j+1}.

Therefore
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for the Hankel operator
H_{F_{j+1}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|=t_{j+1}.
∎

Proposition 10.13
.

Let

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1},\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1})

be a Schmidt pair for
T_{j+1}
corresponding to
t_{j+1}
for some
v_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n}),

w_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\perp}.
Let

x_{j+1}=(I_{n}-\xi_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}-\cdots-\xi_{j}\xi_{j}^{*})v_{j+1},\quad y_{j+1}=(I_{m}-\bar{\eta}_{0}\eta_{0}^{T}-\cdots-\bar{\eta}_{j}\eta_{j}^{T})w_{j+1},

and let

\hat{x}_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{y}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}.

(10.72)

Then

\begin{array}[]{lll}\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{j}(z)\wedge v_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}}&=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\dots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j}(z)\wedge w_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{j+i}(z)|,\\
\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}&=\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|,\;\text{and}\\
\|x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}&=\|y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|,\end{array}

(10.73)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Proof.

By Lemma
10.12
,
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j+1}}
corresponding to
\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|=t_{j+1}
. Hence

H_{F_{j+1}}\hat{x}_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{y}_{j+1}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{F_{j+1}}^{*}\hat{y}_{j+1}=t_{j+1}\hat{x}_{j+1}.

By Theorem
4.11
,

t_{j+1}\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=\|H_{F_{j+1}}\|\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Thus

\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}

(10.74)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Notice that
\bar{A}_{j}(z)
is isometric for almost every
z\in\mathbb{T},
and therefore, by equations (
10.72
), we obtain

\|x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}.

Moreover, since
B_{j}(z)
are isometries almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
by equations (
10.72
), we have

\|y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
By equations (
10.74
), we deduce

\|x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}

(10.75)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

By Proposition
5.1
,

\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}x_{j+1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\dot{\wedge}v_{j+1}

(10.76)

and

\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}y_{j+1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}w_{j+1}.

(10.77)

Hence, by Proposition
3.14
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{j}(z)\wedge v_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}}\\
&=\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{j}(z)\wedge x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}},\\
&=\|x_{j+1}(z)-\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\langle x_{j+1}(z),\xi_{i}(z)\rangle\xi_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}},\end{array}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
. Furthermore

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j}(z)\wedge w_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j}(z)\wedge y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m}}\\
&=\|y_{j+1}(z)-\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{j}\langle y_{j+1}(z),\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\rangle\bar{\eta}_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}},\end{array}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
.

Thus, by equation (
10.75
),

\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j}(z)\wedge w_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m}}=\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{j}(z)\wedge v_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Recall that
h_{j+1}
is the scalar outer factor of
\xi_{0}\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{j}\wedge v_{j+1}
. Hence

\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}=\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|,

\|x_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|y_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|,

and

\|\xi_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\xi_{j}(z)\wedge v_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\wedge\cdots\wedge\bar{\eta}_{j}(z)\wedge w_{j+1}(z)\|_{\wedge^{j+2}\mathbb{C}^{m}}\|=|h_{j+1}(z)|,

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
∎

Proposition 10.14
.

In the notation of Theorem
10.10
, there exist unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{j+1},\tilde{W}_{j+1}
of types
(n-j-1)\times(n-j-2)
and
(m-j-1)\times(m-j-2)
respectively of the form

\tilde{V}_{j+1}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{j}\xi_{j+1}&\bar{\alpha}_{j+1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}&\bar{\beta}_{j+1}\par\end{pmatrix},

where
\alpha_{j+1},\beta_{j+1}
are inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous and all minors on the first columns of
\tilde{V}_{j+1},\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{T}
are in
H^{\infty}.
Furthermore, the set
\mathcal{E}_{j+1}
of all level
j+1
superoptimal error functions for
G
is equal to the following set

\scriptsize{W_{0}^{*}\cdots\begin{pmatrix}I_{j+1}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{*}\par\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&0&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&0&0\\
\vdots&\hskip 24.11108pt\ddots&~&\vdots\\
0&0&t_{j+1}u_{j+1}&0\\
0&0&0&(F_{j+2}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j+1})\par\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I_{j+1}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{j+1}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\cdots V_{0}^{*},}

where
F_{j+2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)}),

u_{j+1}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{j+1}}{h_{j+1}}
is a quasi-continuous unimodular function and
B(t_{j+1})
is the closed ball of radius
t_{j+1}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)}).

Proof.

Recall that, in diagrams (
10.50
) and (
10.56
), the operators

M_{\bar{A}_{j}},\;M_{B_{j}},

(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\xi_{j}\cdot)
and
(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\cdots\dot{\wedge}\bar{\eta}_{j}\dot{\wedge}\cdot)
are unitaries. Since both diagrams commute and
(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})
defined above is a Schmidt pair for
\Gamma_{j+1}
corresponding to
t_{j+1},
by Lemma
10.12
,
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
is a Schmidt pair for
H_{F_{j+1}}
corresponding to
t_{j+1},
where

\hat{x}_{j+1}=A_{j}x_{j+1},\quad\hat{y}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{*}y_{j+1}.

We intend to apply Lemma
4.12
to
H_{F_{j+1}}
and the Schmidt pair
(\hat{x}_{j+1},\hat{y}_{j+1})
to find unitary-valued functions
\tilde{V}_{j+1},\tilde{W}_{j+1}
such that, for every
\tilde{Q}_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
which is at minimal distance from
F_{j+1},
we obtain a factorisation of the form

F_{j+1}-\tilde{Q}_{j+1}=\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{j+1}u_{j+1}&0\\
0&F_{j+2}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{j+1}^{*},

for some
F_{j+2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)}).
For this purpose we find the inner-outer factorisations of
\hat{x}_{j+1}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j+1}.

By Proposition
10.13
,

\|\hat{x}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|

(10.78)

and

\|\hat{y}_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=|h_{j+1}(z)|,

(10.79)

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.

Equations (
10.78
) and (
10.79
) imply that
h_{j+1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})
is the scalar outer factor of both
\hat{x}_{j+1}
and
\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j+1}.

Hence, by Lemma
4.12
,

\hat{x}_{j+1},\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j+1}
admit the inner outer factorisations

\hat{x}_{j+1}=\hat{\xi}_{j+1}h_{j+1},\quad\bar{z}\bar{y}_{j+1}=\hat{\eta}_{j+1}h_{j+1},

for some inner
\hat{\xi}_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}),\hat{\eta}_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}).
Then

\hat{x}_{j+1}=\hat{\xi}_{j+1}h_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1},\quad\bar{z}\bar{\hat{y}}_{j+1}=\hat{\eta}_{j+1}h_{j+1}=\bar{z}B_{j}^{T}\bar{y}_{j+1},

from which we obtain

\hat{\xi}_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}\xi_{j+1},\quad\hat{\eta}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}.

We wish to show that
A_{j}^{T}\xi_{j+1},\;B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}
are inner functions in order to apply Lemma
4.12
. Observe that, by equations (
10.61
) and (
10.62
),

x_{j+1}=\bar{A}_{j}A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1},\quad y_{j+1}=B_{j}B_{j}^{*}w_{j+1}.

Then,

A_{j}^{T}x_{j+1}=A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1},\quad B_{j}^{T}\bar{y}_{j+1}=B_{j}^{T}\bar{w}_{j+1},

and since

\xi_{j+1}=\frac{x_{j+1}}{h_{j+1}},\quad\eta_{j+1}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{y}_{j+1}}{h_{j+1}},

the functions

A_{j}^{T}\xi_{j+1}=\frac{A_{j}^{T}v_{j+1}}{h_{j+1}},\quad B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}=\frac{B_{j}^{*}w_{j+1}}{h_{2}}

are analytic. Furthermore, by Proposition
5.1

\|\xi_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=1
and
\|\eta_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}=1
almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T},
and, by equations (
10.73
),

\|A_{j}^{T}(z)\xi_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n-j-1}}=1,\quad\|B_{j}^{T}(z)\eta_{j+1}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m-j-1}}=1

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}.
Thus
A_{j}^{T}\xi_{j+1},\;B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}
are inner functions.

By Lemma
4.12
, there exist inner, co-outer, quasi-continuous functions
\alpha_{j+1},\beta_{j+1}
of types
(n-j-1)\times(n-j-2),(m-j-1)\times(m-j-2)
respectively such that the functions

\tilde{V}_{j+1}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{j}^{T}\xi_{j+1}&\bar{\alpha}_{j+1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}B_{j}^{T}\eta_{j+1}&\bar{\beta}_{j+1}\par\end{pmatrix}

are unitary-valued with all minors on the first columns in
H^{\infty}.
Furthermore, by Lemma
4.12
, every
\hat{Q}_{j+1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)})
which is at minimal distance from
F_{j+1}
satisfies

F_{j+1}-\hat{Q}_{j+1}=\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{j+1}u_{j+1}&0\\
0&F_{j+2}\end{pmatrix}\tilde{V}_{j+1}^{*},

for some
F_{j+2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)})
, where
u_{j+1}
is a quasi-continuous unimodular function given by
u_{j+1}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{j+1}}{h_{j+1}}.

By Lemma
4.15
, the set

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{j+1}=\{F_{j+1}-\hat{Q}:\hat{Q}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-1)\times(n-j-1)}),\|F_{j+1}-\hat{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}=t_{j+1}\}

satisfies

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{j+1}=\tilde{W}_{j+1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{{}_{j+1}}u_{{}_{j+1}}&0\\
0&(F_{j+2}+H^{\infty})\cap B(t_{j+1})\end{pmatrix}V_{j+1}^{*},

where
B(t_{j+1})
is the closed ball of radius
t_{j+1}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j-2)\times(n-j-2)}).
Thus, by Proposition
10.1
,
\mathcal{E}_{j+1}
admits the factorisation claimed.∎

Theorem 10.15
.

Let
G\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n})+C(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n}).
Let
T_{i},x_{i},y_{i},h_{i}
, for
i\geq 0
, be defined by the algorithm from
Subsection
4.2
. Let
r
be the least index
j\geq 0
such that
T_{j}=0
. Then
r\leq\min(m,n)
and the superoptimal approximant
\mathcal{A}G
is given by the formula

G-\mathcal{A}G=\displaystyle\sum\limits_{0}^{r-1}\frac{t_{i}y_{i}x_{i}^{*}}{|h_{i}|^{2}}.

Proof.

First observe that, if
T_{0}=H_{G}=0,
then this implies
G\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{m\times n}),
and so

\mathcal{A}G=G.

Otherwise, let
t_{0}=\|H_{G}\|>0.

If
T_{1}=0
, by Theorem
9.1
,

H_{F_{1}}=0,
that is,

F_{1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-1)\times(n-1)}).

Then, by Lemma
4.15
, we have

W_{0}(G-\mathcal{A}G)V_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

Equivalently

\begin{array}[]{cllll}G-\mathcal{A}G&=W_{0}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}V_{0}^{*}\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{0}&\beta_{0}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}^{*}\\
\alpha_{0}^{T}\end{pmatrix}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{lll}&=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{0}t_{0}u_{0}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{0}^{*}\\
\alpha_{0}^{T}\end{pmatrix}\vskip 8.61108pt=\bar{\eta}_{0}t_{0}u_{0}\xi_{0}^{*}\\
&=t_{0}\displaystyle\frac{zy_{0}}{\bar{h}_{0}}\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h_{0}}}{h_{0}}\frac{x_{0}^{*}}{\bar{h}_{0}}\vskip 8.61108pt=\displaystyle\frac{t_{0}y_{0}x_{0}^{*}}{|h_{0}|^{2}}.\par\par\end{array}

Let
j
be a non-negative integer such that
T_{j}=0
and
T_{i}\neq 0
for
1\leq i<j
. By the commutativity of the diagrams (
10.50
) and (
10.56
),
H_{F_{j}}=0
, and therefore
F_{j}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{(m-j)\times(n-j)})
.
By Proposition
10.14
, the superoptimal analytic approximant
\mathcal{A}G
satisfies equation (
10.5
), that is,

G-\mathcal{A}G=W_{0}^{*}W_{1}^{*}\cdots W_{j-1}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}t_{0}u_{0}&0&\dots&0\\
0&t_{1}u_{1}&\dots&0\\
\vdots&\hskip 43.05542pt\ddots&&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&t_{j-1}u_{j-1}&0\\
0&\dots&\dots&0\end{pmatrix}V_{j-1}^{*}\cdots V_{1}^{*}V_{0}^{*},

(10.80)

where, for
i=0,1,\dots,j-1
,

\tilde{V}_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\alpha_{0}^{T}\xi_{i}&\bar{\alpha}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{W}_{i}^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{i-1}^{T}\cdots\beta_{0}^{T}\eta_{i}&\bar{\beta}_{i}\end{pmatrix}

are unitary-valued functions, as described in Proposition
10.4
,

u_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{h}_{i}}{h_{i}}
are quasi-continuous unimodular functions, and

V_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{V}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad W_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{i}&0\\
0&\tilde{W}_{i}\end{pmatrix}.

Recall that, by equations (
4.41
), for
i=0,\dots,j-1
,

\xi_{i}=\frac{x_{i}}{h_{i}},\quad\eta_{i}=\frac{\bar{z}\bar{y}_{i}}{h_{i}}.

(10.81)

By Proposition
10.13
, for
i=0,\dots,j-1
,

|h_{i}(z)|=\|x_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}=\|y_{i}(z)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}

almost everywhere on
\mathbb{T}
.

With the aid of the formulae (
10.13
) and (
10.15
), equation (
10.80
)
simplifies to

\displaystyle G-\mathcal{A}G=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{t_{0}y_{0}x_{0}^{*}}{|h_{0}|^{2}}+t_{1}\frac{1}{|h_{1}|^{2}}B_{0}B_{0}^{*}y_{1}x_{1}^{*}\bar{A}_{0}A_{0}^{T}+\dots

\displaystyle\;+t_{j-1}\frac{1}{|h_{j-1}|^{2}}B_{j-1}B_{j-1}^{*}y_{j-1}x_{j-1}^{*}\bar{A}_{j-1}A_{j-1}^{T}.

(10.82)

By equations (
10.69
) and (
10.71
), for
i=0,\dots,j-1
,

x_{i}^{*}=x_{i}^{*}\bar{A}_{i-1}A_{i-1}^{T}\;\;\text{and}\;\;y_{i}=B_{i-1}B_{i-1}^{*}y_{i}.

Thus

G-\mathcal{A}G=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{r-1}\frac{t_{i}y_{i}x_{i}^{*}}{|h_{i}|^{2}}

and the assertion has been proved. ∎

11.
Application of the algorithm

Let us now apply the new algorithm to the example Peller and Young solved in
[
27
]
.

Problem 11.1
.

Let
G=B^{-1}A\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
where

A(z)=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}+2z\hskip 8.61108pt&0\\
0\hskip 8.61108pt&1\par\end{pmatrix},\;\;B(z)=\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}z^{2}\hskip 8.61108ptz\\
z\hskip 8.61108pt-1\par\end{pmatrix}\qquad\mbox{ for all }z\in\mathbb{T}.

Find the superoptimal singular values of
G
and its superoptimal approximant
\mathcal{A}G\in H^{\infty},
that is, the unique
\mathcal{A}G\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
such that the sequence

s^{\infty}(G-\mathcal{A}G)=(s_{0}^{\infty}(G-\mathcal{A}G),s_{1}^{\infty}(G-\mathcal{A}G),\cdots)

is lexicographically minimized.

On
\mathbb{T}

G
is

G(z)=\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{3}\bar{z}^{2}+2\bar{z}\hskip 8.61108pt&\bar{z}\\
\sqrt{3}\bar{z}+2\hskip 8.61108pt&-1\end{pmatrix}.

Step 0:

The operator
H_{G}^{*}H_{G}
with the respect to the orthonormal basis

B=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}z\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
z\end{pmatrix}\right\}

of
(z^{2}H^{2})^{\perp},

has matrix representation

H^{*}_{G}H_{G}\sim\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}&10\hskip 8.61108pt&2\sqrt{3}\hskip 8.61108pt&2\hskip 8.61108pt&0\\
&2\sqrt{3}\hskip 8.61108pt&3\hskip 8.61108pt&\sqrt{3}\hskip 8.61108pt&0\\
&2\hskip 8.61108pt&\sqrt{3}\hskip 8.61108pt&1\hskip 8.61108pt&0\\
&0\hskip 8.61108pt&0\hskip 8.61108pt&0\hskip 8.61108pt&0\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Then
\|H_{G}\|=\sqrt{6}
and a non-zero vector
x_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})
such that

\|H_{G}x_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\perp}}=\|H_{G}\|\|x_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})}

is

x_{0}(z)=\begin{pmatrix}4+\sqrt{3}z\\
1\end{pmatrix}.

For
(x_{0},y_{0})
to be a Schmidt pair for
H_{G}
corresponding to
\|H_{G}\|,
the vector
y_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\perp}
can be calculated by

y_{0}(z)=\frac{H_{G}x_{0}(z)}{\|H_{G}\|}=\displaystyle 2\bar{z}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{z}+\sqrt{3}\\
1\end{pmatrix}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\perp}.

Perform the inner-outer factorizations

x_{0}=\xi_{0}h_{0},\quad\bar{z}\bar{y}_{0}=\eta_{0}h_{0}

for some inner
\xi_{0},\eta_{0}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})
and some scalar outer
h_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}).

In this example

\xi_{0}(z)=\frac{x_{0}}{h_{0}}=\frac{a}{4\sqrt{3}(1-\gamma z)}\begin{pmatrix}4+\sqrt{3}z\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)=\frac{zy_{0}}{\bar{h}_{0}}=\frac{2a}{4\sqrt{3}(1-\gamma\bar{z})}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{z}+\sqrt{3}\\
1\end{pmatrix},

where

h_{0}(z)=\frac{4\sqrt{3}}{a}(1-\gamma z),

a=\sqrt{10-2\sqrt{13}}
and
\gamma=-\frac{a^{2}}{4\sqrt{3}}.

A function
Q_{1}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
that satisfies

(G-Q_{1})x_{0}=t_{0}y_{0},\quad(G-Q_{1})^{*}y_{0}=t_{0}x_{0}

is

Q_{1}(z)=\begin{pmatrix}0&\sqrt{6}\\
2\sqrt{2}&-\sqrt{6}(z+\sqrt{3})\end{pmatrix}.

Step 1:

Let
X_{1}=\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})
and
Y_{1}=\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\perp}.

Let
T_{1}:X_{1}\to Y_{1}
be given by

T_{1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}x)=P_{Y_{1}}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}(G-Q_{1})x)

for all
x\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2}).

Note that

\begin{array}[]{cll}X_{1}&=\left\{\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
f_{2}\end{pmatrix}\;:\;f_{i}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})\right\}\\
&=\left\{\displaystyle\frac{a}{4\sqrt{3}}\frac{(4+\sqrt{3}z)f_{2}-f_{1}}{1-\gamma z}\;:\;f_{i}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})\right\}.\end{array}

If we choose

f_{1}=-\frac{4\sqrt{3}}{a}(1-\gamma z)g\quad\text{and}\quad f_{2}=0

for some
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}),
we obtain
X_{1}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}).

Also

\begin{array}[]{cll}Y_{1}&=\left\{\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{z}\bar{\varphi}_{1}\\
\bar{z}\bar{\varphi}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\;:\;\varphi_{i}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})\right\}\\
&=\left\{\displaystyle\frac{a\bar{z}}{2\sqrt{3}}\frac{(\bar{z}+\sqrt{3})\bar{\varphi}_{2}-\bar{\varphi}_{1}}{1-\gamma\bar{z}}\;:\;\varphi_{i}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})\right\}.\end{array}

If we choose

\varphi_{1}=-\frac{2\sqrt{3}}{a}(1-\gamma z)\psi,\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{2}=0

for some
\psi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}),
we find that
Y_{1}=H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})^{\perp}.

We have

T_{1}\left(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
f_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)=T_{1}\left(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{4\sqrt{3}}{a}(1-\gamma z)g\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right)=\frac{u(\gamma)}{z-\gamma}

where

u(\gamma)=\sqrt{2}(1-\gamma^{2})(2\sqrt{3}\gamma+1)g(\gamma).

Then
t_{1}=\|T_{1}\|=\sqrt{2}(4-\sqrt{13}).

Since
T_{1}
is a compact operator, there exist
v_{1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2}),

w_{1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\perp}
such that

T_{1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}v_{1})=t_{1}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}w_{1}),\quad T_{1}^{*}(\bar{\eta}_{0}\dot{\wedge}w_{1})=t_{1}(\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}v_{1}).

Here we can choose

v_{1}(z)=\frac{4\sqrt{3}}{a}\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad w_{1}(z)=\frac{2\sqrt{3}}{a}\bar{z}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Perform the inner-outer factorisation of
\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}v_{1}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{D},\wedge^{2}\mathbb{C}^{2}).
The function
h_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{1-\gamma z}
is the scalar outer factor of
\xi_{0}\dot{\wedge}v_{1}.

Let

x_{1}=(I-\xi_{0}(z)\xi_{0}^{*}(z))v_{1}(z),\quad y_{1}(z)=(I-\bar{\eta}_{0}(z)\eta_{0}^{T}(z))w_{1}(z).

Then

x_{1}=\frac{\gamma}{\alpha}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{-4\sqrt{3}}{\gamma}(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})-19-4\sqrt{3}(z+\bar{z})\\
-4-\sqrt{3}\bar{z}\end{pmatrix}

and

y_{1}=\frac{2\gamma\bar{z}}{\alpha}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\gamma}(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})+4+\sqrt{3}(z+\bar{z})\\
z+\sqrt{3}\end{pmatrix}.

Calculations yield

x_{1}=\frac{\gamma}{\alpha}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-4-\sqrt{3}\bar{z}\end{pmatrix},\quad y_{1}=\frac{2\gamma\bar{z}}{\alpha}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma z)(1-\gamma\bar{z})}\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
z+\sqrt{3}\end{pmatrix}.

The algorithm stops after at most
\min(m,n)
steps, hence in this case after
2
steps. Then, by Theorem
10.10
, the unique analytic superoptimal approximant
\mathcal{A}G
is given by the formula

\mathcal{A}G=G-\frac{t_{0}y_{0}x_{0}^{*}}{|h_{0}|^{2}}-\frac{t_{1}y_{1}x_{1}^{*}}{|h_{1}|^{2}}.

All terms of
\mathcal{A}G
can be calculated now, to give

\mathcal{A}G=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{1-\gamma z}\begin{pmatrix}-\gamma&\sqrt{3}+4\gamma\\
2+\gamma\sqrt{3}-\gamma z&-(\sqrt{3}+4\gamma)(\sqrt{3}+z)\end{pmatrix},

which is the
unique superoptimal analytic approximant
for the given
G
in Problem
11.1
.
